## Pancho power profile

- **Panel:** 20W, 12V nominal → Vmpp ~17V, Voc ~21-22V
- **Battery:** 2S 18650 Li-Ion → full charge 8.4V, nominal 7.4V
- **Loads:** RPi (~3-5W) + ESP32-CAM (~0.5W) + LEDs (peak flash) + audio
- **Total load:** probably 5-7W continuous, flash peaks on top

From 20W panel: ~13-15W available for charging after load, so realistic charge current is around **1-1.5A** into the 8.4V battery.

---

## Recommended IC: LT3652 (same family)

The standard LT3652 handles up to **32V input** — which covers the 12V panel Voc of ~21V comfortably. Same IC, same integrated switch, just different resistor values.

| Parameter | Daffodil | Pancho |
|---|---|---|
| Panel | USB 5V regulated | Raw 20W, 17V Vmpp |
| VIN range | 5-6V | 17-22V |
| Battery | 1S LiFePO4, 3.6V | 2S Li-Ion, 8.4V |
| VFLOAT set | ~3.6V via divider | ~8.4V via divider |
| MPPT needed | No (panel regulated) | **Yes — critical** |
| IC | LT3652 (C671115) | Same LT3652 |

The VIN_REG MPPT setpoint changes to ~15V (just below Vmpp so panel doesn't collapse):
```
VMPPT = 2.7V × (1 + R_M1/R_M2)
15V: R_M1=455k, R_M2=100k  → use 470k/100k = 15.7V
```

---

## Power architecture for Pancho

The bigger design question is what feeds the RPi and ESP32-CAM:

```
Solar (17V) → LT3652 → 2S battery (8.4V)
                              │
                    ┌─────────┼─────────┐
                    │         │         │
              Buck 5V      Buck 5V    Buck 3.3V
             (RPi, 2-3A)  (cameras)  (ESP32-CAM)
```

For the 5V buck at 2-3A you'll need something like an **MP2307** or **XL4016** — the LT3652 only handles the battery charging side.

---

## One concern: RPi + solar

RPi draws unpredictably (SD card writes, CPU spikes). Combined with flash LEDs, the battery can sag fast. Two 18650 at ~6Ah gives decent headroom, but you'll want to monitor battery voltage and have a graceful shutdown on the RPi before it drops below 6.0V (2×3.0V cutoff).

Is that something you've thought about, or is that a question for later?

--- CLAUDE ---
That simplifies Pancho considerably then. The shield just needs:
- Camera connectors (2×)
- LED flash circuit
- Mic + speaker
- Serial header to ESP32
- Power rails received from the ESP32 board

The LT3652 charging question belongs to the **ESP32 power board** (the equivalent of Wally, but upgraded for 2S 18650 + 20W raw panel). That's the board that needs the charger.

So for Pancho the shield, the main design questions are:

1. **What cameras?** — CSI ribbon (RPi native, no USB overhead) vs USB cameras
2. **Flash LEDs** — high current pulse, needs a FET gate driven by ESP32 GPIO or a dedicated LED driver
3. **Mic** — I2S (e.g. INMP441) or analogue via ADC?
4. **Speaker** — I2S amp (e.g. MAX98357A) directly from RPi I2S? Or analogue?
5. **Serial** — UART between RPi GPIO14/15 and ESP32, level shift if needed (RPi is 3.3V, ESP32 also 3.3V so probably direct)

And the ESP32 power board needs the LT3652 (or similar) for charging — but that's a separate PCB design conversation.

Is Pancho the shield your immediate focus, or are you designing both boards together?

2. Key Technical Concepts:
   - **Build 8 hardware changes (Daffodil):** Removed Q4 (MMBTRA104SS PNP), R17 repurposed as 10k pull-up (V33→ME2149 CE), R18=330Ω series from GPIO32, ADS1115 ch3 moved from V50 to V50_I, diode removed from MCP73123, D1 LED (LCSC C965793, 0402) + R27 (1.2k) added to MCP73123 STAT pin
   - **GPIO32 CE control math:** GPIO32=LOW → CE = 3.3×330/(10000+330) = 0.106V (ME2149 off); GPIO32=HIGH → CE = 3.3V (ME2149 on). 10k pull-up means ME2149 defaults ON at boot (safe).
   - **V50 vs V50_I:** V50 is always live (solar OR ME2149 output). V50_I is solar-panel-direct (0V at night). MCP73123 connected to V50_I in Rev 8 — this already solves circular charging: V50_I=0V at night → MCP73123 UVLO (4.05V) → inactive → no circular loop
   - **Circular charging 41% loss (now resolved):** Original problem was MCP73123 on V50 (battery→ME2149→V50→MCP73123→battery loop, 0.90×0.66=59% efficiency = 41% wasted). Rev 8 moves MCP73123 to V50_I, eliminating this.
   - **CN3801 confirmed WRONG for LiFePO4:** User verified from JLCPCB page — CN3801 does not support LiFePO4. It is a LiPo (4.2V) charger. All previous CN3801 circuit values are invalid for this application.
   - **LT3652 (JLCPCB C671115):** Buck MPPT charger, VIN max 32V (covers 12V panel Voc ~21V), integrated power switch (no external FETs), programmable float voltage, MPPT via VIN_REG. Recommended for Pancho (raw 20W solar + 2S 18650). Circular charging avoidance: connects to V50_I (0V at night) → VIN below 4.95V minimum → charger inactive.
   - **USB-regulated panel (Daffodil):** Panel has built-in voltage regulator outputting 5V USB. MPPT is irrelevant — panel handles it. Only remaining LT3652 advantage over MCP73123 is efficiency (240mW vs 840mW heat). Rev 8 MCP73123 on V50_I is already a correct and sound design.
   - **BQ24650 (JLCPCB C88306):** Alternative to LT3652, correct for LiFePO4 via programmable VREG, but requires 2× external N-channel MOSFETs (SI2302DS, C10487). More complex BOM.
   - **STAT pin LED:** MCP73123 STAT is open-drain, pulls LOW when charging. V50→R27(1.2k)→D1(LED)→STAT. At 5V: I=(5-2)/1200=2.5mA ✓. LED glows at night during any circular charging scenario (indicator only).
   - **Pancho = E2M device:** Eyes, Ears and Mouth. RPi + ESP32-CAM AI field unit. 20W raw solar, 2S 18650 Li-Ion (8.4V). Pancho PCB is RPi shield only — power managed by separate ESP32 power board.
   - **Naming:** Old Pancho board (ESP32 base) renamed to Wally. New Pancho = E2M RPi+ESP32-CAM device.
   - **LT3652 for Pancho power board:** ICHG=1A via RSENSE=100mΩ; MPPT target ~15V (R_M1=470k, R_M2=100k, ref ~2.7V); VFLOAT=8.4V for 2S Li-Ion (divider formula from datasheet); L1=22µH ≥2A sat; BTST=100nF.
